NHANES 2017-2018 - PUBLIC DATA FILE CATALOG AND COLUMN DICTIONARY
========================================================================
NHANES = National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey, run by the
National Center for Health Statistics (NCHS), part of the US Centers for
Disease Control and Prevention (CDC).

Cycle:        2017-2018 (every file name ends in _J)
Source:       https://wwwn.cdc.gov/nchs/nhanes/continuousnhanes/default.aspx?BeginYear=2017
Local path:   data/raw/nhanes/2017-2018/<file>.xpt
Download:     bash data/sync_nhanes.sh
Contents:     129 public XPT files, 4292 columns (SSEVD_J is withdrawn by CDC and not included)
Codebooks:    https://wwwn.cdc.gov/Nchs/Data/Nhanes/Public/2017/DataFiles/<file>.htm

WHAT IS IN THIS FILE
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Five tables, each between a [TABLE <name>] line and an [END <name>] line:

  glossary       every abbreviation used in this file, the file titles and the
                 CDC column labels, with its meaning
  name_patterns  how NHANES column names are usually built
  categories     25 topic groups
  files          one row per XPT file: component, category, title, column count
  columns        one row per column of every XPT file

Columns of the 'columns' table:
  file            XPT file name without .xpt (as used by nhanesA, e.g. 'DEMO_J')
  position        column order inside the XPT file (1 = first)
  variable        column name exactly as stored in the XPT file
  label_cdc       CDC 'SAS label' exactly as stored in the XPT file (max 40 chars,
                  so CDC abbreviates heavily; matches nhanesA::nhanesTableVars)
  label_expanded  the same label with abbreviations written out; truncated
                  chemical names replaced with the full name from the CDC codebook

Value codes (for example 1 = Male, 7 = Refused, 9 = Don't know) are NOT in this
file. They come from the CDC codebook, e.g. nhanesA::nhanesCodebook('DEMO_J', 'RIAGENDR')
or nhanesA::nhanesTranslate().

FORMAT RULES (for scripts)
------------------------------------------------------------------------
- UTF-8 text. Table cells are separated by ' | ' and padded with spaces: trim every cell.
- The first line after [TABLE <name>] is the header.
- Blank lines inside a table only separate groups for readability: skip them.
- Cells never contain '|'. They can contain '#', quotes and apostrophes, so
  turn off comment and quote handling when parsing.

R:
  read_nhanes_table <- function(name, path = 'data/raw/nhanes/2017-2018/_file_list.txt') {
    x <- readLines(path, encoding = 'UTF-8')
    i <- match(sprintf('[TABLE %s]', name), x); j <- match(sprintf('[END %s]', name), x)
    read.table(text = x[(i + 1):(j - 1)], sep = '|', header = TRUE, strip.white = TRUE,
               quote = '', comment.char = '', stringsAsFactors = FALSE)
  }
  cols <- read_nhanes_table('columns')
  subset(cols, file == 'DEMO_J')        # every column of DEMO_J with its labels

Python:
  def read_nhanes_table(name, path='data/raw/nhanes/2017-2018/_file_list.txt'):
      lines = open(path, encoding='utf-8').read().splitlines()
      body = lines[lines.index(f'[TABLE {name}]') + 1 : lines.index(f'[END {name}]')]
      rows = [[c.strip() for c in l.split('|')] for l in body if l.strip()]
      return [dict(zip(rows[0], r)) for r in rows[1:]]

[TABLE glossary]
group                      | term                                                    | meaning
file naming                | _J                                                      | File suffix for the NHANES 2017-2018 cycle (each 2-year cycle has its own letter)
file naming                | SS (file prefix)                                        | Surplus specimen: lab tests run later on stored leftover blood or urine, usually on a random subsample
file naming                | (Surplus)                                               | Same as SS prefix: results from stored leftover specimens
file naming                | XPT                                                     | SAS transport file format used by CDC to publish NHANES data

survey design              | ACASI                                                   | Audio Computer-Assisted Self-Interview: participant answers sensitive questions privately on a computer
survey design              | Proxy                                                   | Someone else answered for the participant (for example a parent for a child)

survey design              | pseudo-PSU / pseudo-stratum                             | Masked versions of PSU and stratum released for variance estimation
survey design              | Sample weight                                           | Number of people in the US population that a participant represents; needed for national estimates

survey design              | Invalidity code                                         | Scan quality flag in DXA files: tells whether a body region measurement is valid
survey design              | Status code                                             | Tells whether a component was completed, partly completed or not done

COLUMN NAME PATTERNS
========================================================================
Rules of thumb, not guarantees: always check label_cdc for the real meaning.

[TABLE name_patterns]
pattern                | meaning
SEQN                   | Participant ID; join key across all files
WT...                  | Sample weight (for example WTMEC2YR = exam weight, WTSAF2YR = fasting subsample weight)
SDMVPSU, SDMVSTRA      | Survey design variables (pseudo primary sampling unit and stratum) for variance estimation
LBX...                 | Laboratory: measured value
LBD...                 | Laboratory: derived value, converted unit, or comment code
URX... / URD...        | Urine laboratory: measured value / derived value or comment code
SS...                  | Surplus specimen laboratory value
...LC                  | Usually the comment code of a lab value (detection limit flag)
...SI                  | Usually the same lab value in International System of Units
<file prefix>Q###      | Questionnaire item as asked (for example DPQ010)
<file prefix>D###      | Derived or recoded questionnaire item (for example DID040)
<exam prefix>X...      | Examination: measured value (for example BMXWT)
<exam prefix>I...      | Examination: comment on a measurement (for example BMIWT)
<exam prefix>D...      | Examination: derived value or status (for example BMDSTATS)
DR1I... / DR1T...      | Dietary recall day 1: individual food item / day total (DR2 = day 2)
RID..., DMD..., DMQ... | Demographics: screener items, derived demographics, demographic questions
[END name_patterns]


CATEGORIES
========================================================================
[TABLE categories]
category_id | category                                                        | description
01          | Study design and demographics                                   | Participant identifiers, age, sex, race and Hispanic origin, education, income, interview and examination status, and the survey weights needed for national estimates.
02          | Anthropometrics and body composition                            | Height, weight, body mass index, body circumferences, weight history, and dual-energy X-ray absorptiometry scans of bone and soft tissue (not brain imaging).
03          | Cardiovascular and vital signs                                  | Blood pressure readings (manual and oscillometric), pulse, and self-reported blood pressure, cholesterol and cardiovascular health.
04          | Mental health                                                   | Depression screener: the 9-item Patient Health Questionnaire.
05          | Clinical biochemistry and metabolic markers                     | Standard blood chemistry, fasting glucose, insulin, glycated hemoglobin, cholesterol and triglycerides, and the diabetes questionnaire.
06          | Hematology (blood cell counts)                                  | Complete blood count: red cells, white cells and their subtypes, platelets, hemoglobin.
07          | Inflammation markers                                            | High-sensitivity C-reactive protein and alpha-1-acid glycoprotein.
08          | Iron status                                                     | Ferritin, serum iron, iron binding capacity and transferrin receptor.
09          | Vitamins and micronutrients                                     | Folate, vitamins A, C, D and E, carotenoids, and urinary iodine.
10          | Hormones                                                        | Sex steroid hormone panel from stored (surplus) serum.
11          | Kidney and urine measures                                       | Urine albumin and creatinine, urine flow rate, urine pregnancy test, and the kidney and urology questionnaire.
12          | Infectious disease serology                                     | Antibody and antigen tests for hepatitis A to E, human immunodeficiency virus and cytomegalovirus, plus the hepatitis questionnaire.
13          | Tobacco and nicotine                                            | Nicotine metabolites in serum and urine, and questionnaires on smoking, household smokers, recent tobacco use and secondhand smoke.
14          | Metals                                                          | Lead, cadmium, mercury, selenium, manganese, arsenic, chromium, cobalt, nickel and other metals in blood and urine.
15          | Persistent pollutants and industrial chemicals                  | Per- and polyfluoroalkyl substances, flame retardants, phthalates and plasticizers, personal care product chemicals, and ethylene oxide.
16          | Pesticides and herbicides                                       | Organophosphate and pyrethroid insecticide metabolites, herbicides, neonicotinoids, glyphosate, the insect repellent DEET, perchlorate, nitrate and thiocyanate, and the pesticide use questionnaire.
17          | Volatile organic compounds and polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons | Volatile organic compounds in blood, their metabolites in urine, polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbon metabolites in urine, and the volatile toxicant questionnaire.
18          | Diet, supplements and food security                             | 24-hour dietary recall (foods and nutrient totals, two days), food code descriptions, dietary supplement use, diet behavior, and food security.
19          | Hearing                                                         | Audiometry exam (hearing thresholds, tympanometry, acoustic reflex, wideband reflectance) and the hearing questionnaire.
20          | Oral health                                                     | Dental exam findings, dental care recommendations and the oral health questionnaire.
21          | Liver                                                           | Liver stiffness and fat content measured by ultrasound transient elastography.
22          | Physical activity, sleep and functioning                        | Physical activity (adults and youth), physical functioning and sleep.
23          | Medical conditions, medications and preventive care             | Self-reported conditions, prescription medications, aspirin use, osteoporosis, immunization, skin, reproductive health, disability and general health status.
24          | Alcohol and drug use                                            | Alcohol use and drug use questionnaires.
25          | Healthcare access, insurance, housing and socioeconomic         | Health insurance, hospital use and access to care, income, occupation, housing, early childhood, acculturation and consumer behavior.
[END categories]
